# Planned illustrations

275 unique illustrations, generated by `tools/art_list.py` from the catalog, subject plan, briefs and `config/art_style.json`. Uniqueness is enforced by `tools/art_catalog.py`. Status marks: ✓ approved, ◐ received or proposed and awaiting review, ★ owner-authored prompt.

## Shared template

Every prompt has these sections, in order:
1. title;
2. introduction;
3. format;
4. composition;
5. subject details;
6. activity;
7. source text (verbatim flavour);
8. style and atmosphere;
9. final requirements;
10. style references.

The lines below are shared. Each entry lists only its own lines.

**Introduction:** Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of {identity}, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.

**Format, scenes:** {aspect_words} composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing. The illustration must have a natural, irregular, hand-drawn white vignette around all four edges. The artwork gradually dissolves into blank ivory-white paper through increasingly sparse pen strokes, broken outlines and diminishing hatching. Leave approximately 5-8% clean white margin around the perimeter, with an additional organic transition zone. Avoid digital blur, artificial feathering, or hard rectangular cropping. Keep crisp line and strong contrast at the focal point.

**Format, figures:** {aspect_words} composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing. A single subject drawn whole, isolated on blank ivory-white paper: only a small patch of ground beneath it and its cast shadow, which fade out in a few sparse strokes. No background scenery. Leave at least 8% clean white margin on every side; nothing touches or is cropped by the image edge. The silhouette must read clearly when printed about two inches wide.

**Format, still lifes:** {aspect_words} composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing. A still-life arrangement isolated on blank ivory-white paper, resting on a small surface that fades out in a few sparse strokes. No figure and no background scenery. Leave at least 8% clean white margin on every side; nothing touches or is cropped by the image edge.

**Style, scenes:** Draw it with confident hand-inked line: clean outlines of varied weight made with dip pen and brush, carrying the slight natural irregularity of a human hand. Build the picture from clearly drawn shapes, not surface texture. Use hatching and crosshatching selectively, only to model form and shadow, and keep generous areas of open white paper; let distance thin to simple, lighter outline. Put detail where the eye should land and suggest the rest with a few telling strokes. No stipple fields, no noise-like texture, no tonal haze, no over-rendered surfaces. Accurate perspective, believable construction and coherent geography; the world shows withered beauty: once-grand things rusted, cracked, overgrown or hollowed by age. It must look drawn by hand, not generated or rendered.

**Style, figures and still lifes:** Ink it like classic rulebook monster and character art: a bold, confident outer contour, clean interior lines, hatching only where form turns into shadow, and a few solid-black spot shapes for the deepest darks. Large areas stay open white paper. Simple, readable shapes over surface texture: no stippled skin, no noise, no over-rendered detail. Light falls from the upper left. Believable anatomy, armour construction, weapon grips and materials, worn and weathered. It must look drawn by hand with pen, brush and ink, not generated or rendered.

**Final, scenes:** Avoid photorealism, digital-render polish, hyper-detail, uniform texture everywhere, painterly shading, smooth gradients, grey wash, colour, typography, readable runes, watermarks, signatures, modern objects, artificial symmetry and competing focal points. The finished artwork should read cleanly and print crisply, with an organically fading white vignette, like original ink art in a professionally printed fantasy rulebook.

**Final, figures and still lifes:** Avoid photorealism, digital-render polish, hyper-detail, stippled or noisy texture, painterly shading, smooth gradients, grey wash, colour, typography, readable runes, watermarks, signatures, modern objects, cartoon or anime proportions and cropped limbs. The finished artwork should be clean, bold and readable at small size, like original ink art in a professionally printed fantasy rulebook.

**References:** The attached reference images are hand-inked art by human illustrators. Match their line quality, economy of line, hatching density, amount of open white paper and finish, but do not copy their subjects or compositions.

## Cover (1)

Printed 4.8 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Cinematic establishing scene with one clear focal group; open white sky in the upper third reserved for the typeset title; strong black foreground framing. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. Front Cover

`ART-COVER-002`

```text
FRONT COVER - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Recommended First Party of Heraldry Skirmish (Human Fighter, Elf Ranger, Dwarf Cleric, Halfling Rogue), four Heroes on an expedition into the monster-haunted ruins of the Dying Lands while a rival party works the same ground, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
A four-Hero adventuring party — Human Fighter, Elf Ranger, Dwarf Cleric, Halfling Rogue — advancing through a rain-soaked ruined shrine; a rival party's banner across the ruin; a Monster silhouette in the treeline.
Low three-quarter view; party small in the lower third; broken shrine arch as frame; open sky upper half reserved for typeset title.
Low three-quarter view from ground level; the four-Hero party is small in the lower third, advancing between rain-slick flagstones. A broken shrine arch frames the scene on one side and the upper half of the picture is open ruled sky, left empty for the typeset title.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Broken gothic shrine arch with a missing keystone and a headless saint in a wall niche
* Rain drawn as sparse fine diagonal strokes, puddles on the flagstones reflecting the arch
* Human Fighter in a ring shirt with shortsword and small round targe at the front, Elf Ranger in a leather coat with a shortbow, broad Dwarf Cleric in a ring shirt with a mace and small round targe, small Halfling Rogue in a leather coat with a rapier at the hip slipping along the wall edge
* A rival party's tattered banner on a pole planted across the rubble, no readable device
* Fallen columns, toppled statue fragments and moss on the stones
* Treeline at the far edge with one Monster silhouette standing between the trunks, shape only
* Lantern hung on the Ranger's pack, soaked cloaks, packs and bedrolls
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* The four Heroes advance in a loose staggered file, weapons half-drawn, looking toward the banner
* Halfling Rogue crouches at the edge peering at the banner pole
* Monster silhouette stands motionless in the trees, unseen by the party
Wary, rain-heavy and austere; adventurers small against withered grandeur.
Do not draw:
* Do not render the title or any lettering on banners.
* Do not detail the Monster: a dark silhouette only.
* Do not make the party large or heroic-posed; no sunburst or glow.
* Do not give the rival banner a heraldic device that reads as a symbol.
* No plate armour, longbow or staff on the four Heroes.
```

## Chapter openers (11)

Printed 4.5 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Wide establishing scene with a low horizon, black foreground framing and a lighter, finer-lined distance. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. Chapter 1 Opener

`ART-CH01-002`

```text
CHAPTER 1 OPENER - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Dying Lands as a whole, seen from a broken mountain pass: forest on one flank, frozen peaks on the other, and the dead empire's ruined cities sprawled on the eastern horizon, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
The Dying Lands seen from a broken mountain pass: a cracked imperial road winding down toward the skeletal towers of a dead city on the horizon, forest to one side and frozen peaks to the other; a small party with a banner pauses on the road in the foreground.
Wide vista; party small at lower left; road leads the eye to the distant dead city.
Wide vista from a high pass; a cracked imperial road winds down from the lower foreground toward the skeletal towers of a dead city on the horizon. A small party with a banner pauses at lower left, road leading the eye to the city.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Straight, patient imperial road paved in broken slabs, with weeds and a leaning milestone half-swallowed by roots
* Forest mass pressing in on one side, drawn as dense stippled and hatched canopy
* Frozen peaks with fine sparse snow-line hatching on the other side
* Distant dead city of snapped spires, collapsed domes and empty arches in thin fine lines
* Fractured pass rocks and a cairn in the foreground
* Wisps of smoke or ash thinly ruled over the far city
* Sparse horizontal cloud strokes
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Party of four stands on the road at lower left, one holding a banner on a pole, looking down toward the city
* No other figures
Vast, hushed and fatalistic; the world laid out like a wound.
Do not draw:
* Do not repeat the cover composition or the Greythorn bastion portrait; no walled town visible.
* No large heroic party and no legible device on the banner.
* Do not include any monsters or dragons in the sky.
* No panorama of all six climates.
```

### 2. Chapter 2 Opener

`ART-CH02-002`

```text
CHAPTER 2 OPENER - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Two rival adventuring parties of the Dying Lands meeting in a monster-haunted ruin, each fighting for the same objective in a PvPvE skirmish, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Two rival adventuring parties sighting each other across a fog-filled valley of ruined walls at first light, each crouched behind cover with weapons ready, a monster's silhouette moving in the mist between them.
Wide shot; one party at lower left, the other small at far right; fog band across the middle.
Wide shot of a fog-filled valley of ruined walls at first light. One party crouches behind cover at lower left, the second is small at far right, a horizontal fog band crosses the middle and a monster silhouette moves in the mist between them.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Low ruined walls, broken arches and collapsed cottages half-lost in fog
* Fog shown by sparse fine horizontal lines and blank paper
* First light as a few long shadows falling lower right
* Left party behind a toppled wall section, shields and a crossbow barrel just visible
* Right party behind a standing gable end, a banner pole barely visible
* Monster as a large dark silhouette half-dissolved in the mist, ambiguous shape
* Dead tree and a broken cart wheel for scale
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Each party of four crouches or kneels behind cover with weapons ready, watching the other
* Monster slouches through the fog between them, neither party engaging it yet
Tense stillness before violence.
Do not draw:
* Do not show combat actually happening; this is the moment of sighting.
* Do not detail the Monster: a silhouette only, not a portrait.
* Do not repeat the cover or a bridge melee: no rain, no shrine arch, no stone bridge.
* No gore.
```

### 4. Chapter 4 Opener

`ART-CH04-002`

```text
CHAPTER 4 OPENER - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the movement chapter of Heraldry Skirmish, imagined as an expedition crossing a ruined imperial aqueduct over a gorge, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
A party crossing the broken spans of a collapsed aqueduct: one climbs a rope, one leaps a gap, one braces to catch, the gorge falling away below.
Strong diagonal of the broken aqueduct; figures mid-motion; deep drop beneath.
A strong diagonal of a broken aqueduct across the frame, deep gorge falling away beneath. Figures are caught mid-motion on different spans, with the drop dominating the lower half.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Roman-like arched aqueduct with collapsed spans, exposed rubble core and broken parapets
* Ivy and roots hanging from the arches
* Gorge floor far below drawn in fine light lines, a thread of river
* Rope tied off to an arch pier and hanging into a gap
* Loose stones falling from a crumbling edge
* Wind-bent dead trees on the gorge rim
* Packs and a lantern slung on climbers
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* One Hero climbs a rope up a broken span
* One Hero leaps a gap between spans, cloak streaming
* One Hero braces on a solid pier with hand outstretched to catch the others
* A fourth waits further back on the intact section
Vertigo and strain; the world coming apart under their feet.
Do not draw:
* Not a stone bridge melee and not cavern ledges over a chasm: this is an arched aqueduct in open air.
* No monsters.
* Do not depict dimensions or jump distances.
* No lettering on any stones.
```

`ART-CH05-002`

Do not draw:

```

Do not draw:

## World sections (8)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Small establishing scene that captures the section's subject. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. 1.2 The Grim World

`ART-WOR-1-2-THE-GRIM-WORLD`

```text
1.2 THE GRIM WORLD - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Grim World: kings, bishops and warlords raise banners over land they can barely hold, and peasants bear the cost, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Mid-distance view of a burnt farmstead yard with two warlords' banners planted on opposite sides; the tax cart and armed men fill the yard while a peasant family flees down a lane to one side.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Blackened timber farmhouse with a fallen roof and a standing chimney
* Two tall banner poles on either side of the yard with tattered banners of different cut (no legible devices)
* Tax collector's cart with strongbox, ledger and a seated clerk
* Armed men in mismatched gear holding spears, one counting sacks
* Burnt barn, scattered tools and a dead cow or ox
* Fleeing peasant family on the lane with bundles, a child carried, a dog

### 3. 1.3.2 The Six Climates of Ruin

`ART-WOR-1-3-2-THE-SIX-CLIMATES-OF-RUIN`

```text
1.3.2 THE SIX CLIMATES OF RUIN - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the shape of the Dying Lands: broken mountains down the spine, the dead empire in the east, the great forest in the west, ice in the north, dunes in the south and swamps in the south-east, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
A sweeping panorama from the spine of the broken mountains with all six climates under one sky: forest to the west, dead city to the east, ice to the north, dunes to the south and drowned fen to the south-east, with dark cavern mouths in the mountainside below; a lone surveyor with a staff stands in the foreground.
SUBJECT DETAILS

* Western forest as dense hatched mass running to a pale coast
* Eastern city skyline of snapped towers and domes in fine lines
* Northern ice fields with glacial walls and thin hatching
* Southern dunes with tomb-teeth along the horizon
* South-eastern fen of reeds, black pools and drowned trees in sparse fine lines
* Dark cavern mouths and old mine adits in the mountain flank below the ledge (the Undermarches)

HUMAN ACTIVITY

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Beyond the walls, land has become allegiance. Expedition ledgers still say forest, city, cavern, ice, desert, and swamp. Survivors know better. They use older names. The shape of the world is plain enough to anyone who has walked it. The broken mountains run down its spine. The dead empire and its ruined cities sprawl across the eastern heart. The great forest spreads west to the salt-coast, the ice locks the north, the dunes burn in the south beyond the mountain passes, and the swamps drown the south-east where the rivers give out at the sea. The old imperial roads still join all of it, straight and patient, running out from the Ashlands to every climate that survived. A dry label cannot warn a traveller that a road has memory, a court still sits without lungs, or a pool of black water may be listening."
Immense, quiet and unsettled.

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The Greenmark is the great temperate forest of the surviving world. It is larger than the maps. Roots take a road within a season and make the mile-stones useless. Travellers report trees shifting at the edge of sight, upright hounds between trunks, and grove-circles where moss stays warm after snow. Foresters cut warning signs into bark for those who know how to read them. The most common sign means turn back even if the voice ahead is your own. The forest is no wilderness. It is a country that does not recognize human law."
Ancient, watchful and indifferent to humans.

* No letters in the bark signs.

## Bastions (6)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: The town or fortress seen from its approach road, with its defining feature unmistakable and tiny figures for scale. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. Greythorn ✓ ★

`ART-BAS-GREYTHORN`

```text
GREYTHORN - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create an exceptionally detailed, panoramic black-and-white pen-and-ink illustration of Greythorn, a fortified medieval forest town in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish. The artwork should resemble a meticulously hand-drawn illustration from a classic 1970s–1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook.
FORMAT AND WHITE VIGNETTE
Wide 3:2 landscape composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing.
The illustration must have a natural, irregular, hand-drawn white vignette around all four edges. The artwork gradually dissolves into blank ivory-white paper through increasingly sparse pen strokes, broken outlines, diminishing crosshatching, and scattered stippling.
Leave approximately 5–8% clean white margin around the perimeter, with an additional organic transition zone. Avoid digital blur, artificial feathering, or hard rectangular cropping. Preserve crisp detail and strong contrast throughout the central composition.
COMPOSITION AND TOWN SCALE
View Greythorn from an elevated forested hillside, looking down across an ancient woodland road toward the fortified settlement.
Greythorn is a substantial medieval town of approximately 10,000 inhabitants, occupying a broad forest clearing roughly 1–2 km across. Its scale should resemble a significant medieval market town rather than a village, isolated fort, or castle.
The town occupies approximately 70–80% of the central composition's width. Hundreds of visible rooftops extend through successive layers of streets and buildings toward distant palisades, suggesting thousands of structures beyond those individually distinguishable.
The settlement follows gently rising terrain, with dense residential districts, commercial streets, workshops, and open civic spaces. Its distant buildings become progressively smaller and lighter, establishing convincing depth and urban scale.
Two enormous, ancient oak trees frame the foreground, their gnarled trunks, exposed roots, and spreading branches forming a natural canopy without obscuring the town.
A winding forest road leads across a weathered wooden bridge spanning a rocky stream toward the fortified main gate.
GREYTHORN'S FORTIFICATIONS
Greythorn's defensive perimeter consists primarily of tall sharpened timber palisades, reinforced earthworks, elevated wooden fighting platforms, and regularly spaced watchtowers.
A substantial timber gatehouse stands in the middle foreground, with two square defensive towers flanking an open entrance. Guards watch arriving travellers, while rangers patrol the battlements.
The fortifications are functional, weathered, and repeatedly repaired, reflecting a practical frontier settlement rather than an imperial capital.
THE TOWN INTERIOR
Beyond the walls, a dense and architecturally coherent medieval townscape spreads across the clearing.
Buildings consist predominantly of half-timbered houses, workshops, and warehouses with steep shingled or thatched roofs, exposed beams, narrow windows, wooden balconies, and irregular smoking chimneys.
Winding streets, courtyards, small squares, and crowded residential blocks create an organic medieval settlement.
Include several recognizable landmarks:

* A tall central wooden watchtower with a weathered banner.
* A substantial timber council hall.
* An exceptionally large military training yard with archery targets and practising rangers.
* A modest chapel and a small, understated arcane spire.
* A busy crossroads market with merchant stalls and wagons.
* The Hollow Quarter, a secluded lower district incorporating ancient trees, wooden walkways, and small bridges.

Integrate these landmarks naturally into the urban fabric without allowing individual buildings to dominate the overall townscape.
ANCIENT FOREST AND STANDING STONES
Dense primeval woodland surrounds Greythorn. Towering oaks, twisted branches, tangled roots, ferns, moss-covered boulders, and thick undergrowth create the impression of a settlement surviving within an ancient, powerful forest.
In the left foreground, beside the road, place several weathered standing stones carved with spirals and indistinct runes. Small ritual offerings of bundled branches, herbs, and woven baskets rest at their bases, marking the site of Greythorn's annual tithe to the forest.
Subtle supernatural details may reward close inspection, such as an indistinct woodland figure partially concealed among distant trees. Avoid prominent magical effects.
HUMAN ACTIVITY
Populate the roads, bridge, gatehouse, market, and training yard with numerous realistically scaled figures engaged in ordinary activity.
Include cloaked rangers carrying longbows, guards with spears, foresters carrying axes, merchants, burdened horses, supply wagons, apprentices, and townspeople.
Their activities should convey a prosperous but vigilant frontier settlement, without distracting from the architecture and landscape.
ARTISTIC STYLE AND ATMOSPHERE
The atmosphere is grounded, austere, mysterious, and subtly ominous. Greythorn is a resilient bastion of civilization maintained through vigilance and uneasy agreements with the ancient forest.
Use exceptionally fine traditional pen-and-ink rendering, with varied line weights, intricate crosshatching, stippling, textured bark, weathered timber grain, irregular stonework, and carefully constructed rooftops.
Strong foreground contrast and progressively lighter distant detail create atmospheric depth. Maintain accurate perspective, believable construction, and coherent urban geography.
The illustration must feel authentically hand-drawn rather than digitally rendered.
FINAL VISUAL REQUIREMENTS
Prioritize the expansive fortified town and its densely inhabited architecture, framed by monumental ancient oaks. The winding road, wooden bridge, gatehouse, and standing stones establish the foreground narrative.
Avoid elaborate stone castles, exaggerated fantasy towers, artificial symmetry, competing focal points, photorealism, painterly shading, smooth gradients, colour, typography, watermarks, and modern objects.
The finished artwork should be exceptionally detailed yet visually readable, with a convincing medieval urban scale, immersive forest atmosphere, and an organically fading white vignette suitable for seamless reproduction in a professionally printed fantasy rulebook.
```

### 2. Sablekeep

`ART-BAS-SABLEKEEP`

```text
SABLEKEEP - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Sablekeep, a fortified chapel-town built into the throat of a ruined imperial city where the Ashlands meet the eastern forest, ruled by the Sable Watch, which survives by burning, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
View from the straight imperial road looking up at the chapel-town climbing terraces in the throat of the ruined city; braziers burning at every gate, bridge and chapel window.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Terraced chapel-town in dark stone with narrow buttresses and a bell turret
* Lower terraces resting on the bones of an older temple, some arches oddly shaped, almost rib-like, but not clearly bones
* Consecrated braziers on iron stands at gates, bridges and windows, flames drawn as ink strokes
* Ruined imperial city walls and colonnades rising behind
* Eastern forest edge pressing on one flank
* Gate with a bridge over a dry cut and warrior-priests in dark cloaks on watch
* Smoke rising in thin lines
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Warrior-priests tend braziers at the gate, small
* A column of travellers approaches on the road
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Sablekeep is a fortified chapel-town built into the throat of a ruined imperial city, where the Ashlands meet the eastern edge of the forest and the old roads run straight on into the dead empire. It survives by burning. Consecrated braziers stand at every gate, bridge, and chapel window. They are never permitted to go out. The lower terraces rest on the bones of an older temple, and the bones are not all the right shape."
Austere, fanatical vigilance against the dead city.
Do not draw:
* Not a standard castle; a chapel-town crowded on terraces.
* Do not show readable emblems of the Watch.
* No Hearthhold-like central fire or Deepwarden gate.
* No bones shown explicitly as skeletons.
```

`ART-BAS-DEEPWARDEN`

* The Dark below as an abyss of solid black, with a few tiny lantern points

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Deepwarden is a sealed mining city carved into the foothills of the broken mountains. It survived the Fall by closing its great gate. Half the city was on the wrong side when it closed. The half that survived now lives in the upper terraces above what its people call simply the Dark."
Austere, hard and shadowed.
Do not draw:

* No visible creatures in the Dark.
* Not a stone castle on a hill.

## Ancestries (11)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 2:3. Family framing: Single full-length adventurer of this ancestry in travel-worn gear, three-quarter view. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

### 1. Human

`ART-ANC-HUMAN`

```text
HUMAN - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of a Human adventurer, medium-sized, the plain short-lived 'striver' people who persist where steadier peoples fail, shown as a traveller in a Bastion's service, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Full figure in three-quarter view mid-stride on a short patch of rutted track, walking staff planted ahead, head turned back over the shoulder toward unseen pursuit.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Layered road clothes: patched tunic, belted over-coat, hooded mantle pushed back
* Walking staff of plain ash, scuffed iron ferrule
* Short sword in a worn scabbard at the left hip
* Travel pack with bedroll and tin cup lashed on
* Ordinary unremarkable face, stubble, weathered skin, determined set jaw
* Mud-caked boots and frayed hem
* No horns, fur, scales or pointed ears: wholly ordinary proportions
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Humans are short-lived strivers who raise fragile things in a brutal world and call the result civilization. They learn fast, adapt faster, and persist in places that wear steadier peoples down to nothing."
Plain weathered resolve, a small life walking through a vast dying land.
Do not draw:
* No exotic features: the Human must look unmarked beside the other ancestries.
* No armour plate or heroic pose: this is a traveller, not a Fighter.
* Do not copy the Elf's long cloak or the Halfling's hood.
```

`ART-ANC-HALFLING`

Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:

```

Elegiac grace, beauty and loss held back behind composure.

## Backgrounds (20)

Printed 1.6 in wide · aspect 1:1. Family framing: A small still life of the tools, garments and keepsakes of this background's trade; no figure, so it never resembles a class or ancestry portrait. Isolated on white paper; no background scenery.

## Classes (12)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 2:3. Family framing: Single full-length adventurer in an action-ready pose with class-typical arms and gear, three-quarter view. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

### 1. Fighter ✓

`ART-CLA-FIGHTER`

```text
FIGHTER - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of a Fighter, Martial class: drilled, line-holding soldier of fortune; packages of Shortsword, Targe, Ring Shirt, Dagger, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.

SUBJECT DETAILS
* Ring shirt (mail of linked rings) over a padded arming coat
* Targe: small round shield with a central boss, edge dented
* Plain shortsword, single-handed, held ready
* Dagger on belt
* Open-faced helm or coif, no visor
* Stance planted wide, weight forward, knuckles white
* Scuffs and rust on mail, boots braced on a small patch of cracked flagstone
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"A Fighter is forged in places where hesitation gets people killed. Drill, repetition, and hard lessons carve in the instinct to hold ground when holding it is the only thing that matters. They have watched lines collapse, and they know to the heartbeat how long one can stand before it does."
Stoic resolve, the last thing between the line and collapse.

* Not heavy plate: ring shirt only.

## Equipment (2)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Still-life arrangement of the described objects, worn and used. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

### 1. 7.6 Treasure Tables

`ART-ITE-7-6-TREASURE-TABLES`

```text
7.6 TREASURE TABLES - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Treasure in the Dying Lands is never clean: coin and relics come from hoards, vaults and the dead, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Still life on a stone slab at slight elevation: a rotted purse spilling tarnished coins beside a skeletal hand, a mud-caked crown, a cracked reliquary and a salt-crusted signet ring.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Rotted leather purse with a frayed drawstring spilling coins
* Skeletal hand with finger bones curled near the coins
* Mud-caked crown with dull gems and a bent circlet
* Cracked reliquary box with a missing hinge
* Salt-crusted signet ring on a stone
* Coins stippled to feel dull, no readable stamps
* Cobweb, grave dirt and a spilled bone button
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Treasure in the Dying Lands is never clean. Coin comes from burial hoards, failed relief columns, chapel vaults, drowned roads, damned shrines, dead merchants, broken armies, and rooms sealed by people who feared being found. A purse is often the last record of a life. A crown may have passed through dirt, blood, ash, salt water, and another thief's stomach before it reaches a living hand."
Greedy and grave-soiled.
Do not draw:
* Not the Magic traditions still life or the melee weapon still life.
* No readable coin inscriptions.
* No glinting sparkle effects.
* No gore.
```

### 2. 7.8 Melee Weapons

`ART-ITE-7-8-MELEE-WEAPONS`

```text
7.8 MELEE WEAPONS - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Salvaged arms from an older wider world that reach the Dying Lands as trade goods through Saltspire and Reedmarch, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Still life laid out on a sacking cloth viewed from above at an angle: a long-bladed messer, notched swordbreaker, throwing cross, bladed scourge, weighted cord and hook-and-chain, spaced evenly.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Langes messer: long single-edged blade with a nagel-style hilt
* Swordbreaker with a notched back and a heavy guard
* Throwing cross: four-armed bladed star-cross
* Bladed scourge: multiple lashes ending in small blades
* Weighted cord with a heavy ball at one end, coiled
* Hook-and-chain: a long chain ending in a barbed hook
* Burlap sack texture, a trader's tag as a blank rectangle, rope bindings
Must be shown exactly:
* Only the six named weapons
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Many of the arms below carry names from an older, wider world than the Bastions now remember: the long-bladed messer, the notched swordbreaker, the throwing cross, the bladed scourge, the weighted cord, and the hook-and-chain. They reach the Dying Lands as salvage, tribute, and trade goods, carried up the southern routes through Saltspire and bartered onward through Reedmarch. How a blade is named changes nothing about how it cuts. Each is listed here under the name the traders used."
Matter-of-fact salvage with dangerous elegance.
Do not draw:
* Not the treasure still life.
* No weapons beyond the six named, no swords of legend.
* No readable maker's marks.
* No gore or blood.
```

## Spell and power tiers (15)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Small scene vignette of the tradition's magic at work, densest at the caster and thinning into white paper toward irregular edges; no frame.

```text

* Practice post with chopped and scorched marks
* Small flame drawn as a single clean ink flame cupped in the palm
* Air distortion drawn as bent parallel lines before the other palm

* Anxious concentrated face, tongue-tip between teeth
* A battered wooden staff leaning on the post

## Monsters (120)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 1:1. Family framing: Single creature, full body, three-quarter view, in a characteristic menacing pose; a small human figure for scale only if the creature is Large or Huge. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

### 1. Giant Tarantula ◐

`ART-MON-GIANT-TARANTULA`

```text
GIANT TARANTULA - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Giant Tarantula, Greenmark Skirmisher, small: a hand-span of bristled legs around a body the size of a war helm, a forest-floor venomous hunter, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Low three-quarter ground view of the spider stalking across leaf litter on stilted, deliberate legs, forelegs raised, fangs lifted, all eight legs splayed clear of the body so each one can be counted; a discarded war helm lying a short distance to one side for scale, hiding no leg.
Size: Small: smaller than a human.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Exactly eight legs, jointed and tipped with small claws
* Body the size of a war helm, hairy abdomen and cephalothorax
* Bristle hairs suggested by short hatch flicks along the contours, not covering every surface
* Two large fangs with a bead of venom at the tips
* Cluster of eight eyes on a small raised bump
* Palps in front of the fangs
* Leaf litter and a rusted iron helm for scale
* Gripping toe-pads suggesting it can climb walls
Must be shown exactly:
* Exactly eight legs
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"A hand-span of bristled legs around a body the size of a war helm, the giant tarantula stalks the Greenmark's leaf litter on stilted, deliberate steps. Its fangs weep a venom that turns a victim's muscles to wet rope. It spins no snares, preferring to run prey down and pin it where it falls. Foragers keep one eye on the canopy, because the largest specimens hunt from above and drop."
Patient, stilted menace in damp leaf litter.
Do not draw:
* No web: it spins no snares.
* Do not make it larger than a dog.
* No human features.
```

### 2. Goblin

`ART-MON-GOBLIN`

```text
GOBLIN - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Goblin, Greenmark Minion, small: a knee-high green-grey raider of ruined steadings that fights in chittering mobs, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.

Full figure in three-quarter view, hunched and scuttling forward, notched blade out in front, sling and stone pouch at the belt.
Size: Small: smaller than a human.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Knee-high, green-grey (rendered as heavy hatching) wiry body, all elbows and knees
* Large pointed ears, long nose, wide mouth full of crooked teeth
* Notched, jagged short blade in the right hand
* Sling of river stones: leather strap and a bulging pouch of round stones on the belt
* Rag loincloth and strips of stolen leather
* Malicious narrowed eyes
* A few chewed bones on the trampled ground at its feet
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Knee-high, green-grey, and all elbows and malice, goblins infest the Greenmark's ruined steadings in chittering mobs. Each carries a notched blade and a sling of river stones, and not one will hold a line once the fight turns against it. Singly they are cowards; in numbers they are a tide. Greythorn's gate-wardens read goblin-sign in the chewed bones left along the road."
Gleeful, cowardly malice.
Do not draw:
* Not the Goblin ancestry hero: no clean kit, no lantern, no tabard.
* Not the Cave Goblin (blind black eyes) nor Snow Goblin (fur parka).
* Do not give a spear (that is the Cave Goblin).
* No other goblins or mob silhouettes.
```

They were among the first things the burning years shaped, and they have bred true down every generation since."
Feral grievance, an animal with a grudge.

Where beastmen break, orcs hold their formation. A warband takes a steading methodically, gate by gate, and savours the hard fight more than the loot it wins."
Brutal confidence, a warband's anvil.
Do not draw:
* Not the Orc ancestry hero: no tabard, no composed bearing, no clean armour.
* Not a Bugbear (shaggy, long-armed).

## Bosses (15)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: The unique named boss shown with its distinguishing features in its own lair or setting, dramatic in scale; clearly an individual, not a typical member of a kind. Composition reads as a vignette that thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no frame.

### 1. The Ancient Void Dragon

`ART-MON-THE-ANCIENT-VOID-DRAGON`

```text
THE ANCIENT VOID DRAGON - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Ancient Void Dragon, a boss wyrm older than the Fall and perhaps its cause, whose scales drink light and whose breath unmakes substance, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Night sky over a barren crag; the dragon is drawn mostly as negative space, a wyrm-shaped absence of untouched paper edged by fine contour lines, stars dimming around it. A broken watchtower stands small at the bottom for scale.
Size: Huge: towering many times a human's size.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Wyrm silhouette formed by blank paper bounded by thin broken contour lines
* Stars as stippled points thinning and vanishing near its outline
* Spreading wings and a long coiling neck implied by what the stars are missing
* Where the breath passes, the landscape is erased to bare white with abrupt edges
* Barren crag, snapped watchtower and a dry riverbed below
* Solid black used as the surrounding night sky, hatching to form the void contrast
* Peeling, fraying edges of substance along the breath
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Older than the Fall and perhaps its cause, the Ancient Void Dragon is a wound in the shape of a wyrm, its scales drinking the light from the air around them. Its breath neither burns nor freezes but unmakes, peeling away substance until nothing is left. Stars seem to dim where it passes. The wise do not speak its name, and the dead do not speak at all."
Cosmic dread; a hole in the world.
Do not draw:
* Not a normal rendered dragon with scales; absence is the subject.
* Not the Dracolich (bones) or Visath (ice).
* No glow or lens flare.
* No figures except at most one tiny tower.
```

* The child figure sings; no other people
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Survivors describe a small figure at the edge of the torchlight, singing in a tongue that sounds like an old lullaby heard through water. Then the torch goes out. The Voice Beneath the Bells is grief given a child's shape and a drowned chapel's patience, and its song hollows out everyone who hears it. The bells it waits beneath have not rung in a very long time."
Quiet sorrow edged with horror.
Do not draw:

## Scenarios (37)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: The scenario's key place and threat at the moment before action, with small figures for scale. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame. Show the scenario's place and situation as the parties arrive; any named creature, guardian or boss appears only small, partial, distant or in silhouette, so the image is clearly distinct from that creature's bestiary portrait.

### 1. Scenario 1: Hunt the Wild Beast

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-1-HUNT-THE-WILD-BEAST`

```text
SCENARIO 1: HUNT THE WILD BEAST - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of Hunt the Wild Beast, a Greenmark scenario set in a Greythorn logging camp whose palisade timbers have been driven through and splintered by a Great Forest Boar that has made the deep wood its hunting ground, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Wide, slightly raised view of a smashed logging camp at early morning, a game trail leading from its ruined palisade into dense forest in the middle distance. The beast is only a vast dim hump among the trees far back.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Palisade of oak timbers snapped and splintered outward, one trunk driven through a sawpit shed
* Felled logs, stumps, an abandoned two-man saw and ox-yoke
* Cloven hoof furrows and torn earth leading into the trees
* A churned mud wallow glimpsed beyond the camp edge
* A narrow game trail, a pale gap in dense trunks
* Dead dire wolf lying near the camp gate, not gored
* Broken axe handles, a toppled log cart
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Two small hunters with spears and boar-spears crouch at the camp edge reading the tracks
* One kneels to touch a splintered timber
* A watching raven on the palisade
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Something out of the deep wood has taken Greythorn's logging camps for its hunting ground, and with them the timber the Bastion is built on. The first deaths were blamed on dire wolves. The next were found driven through splintered palisade timbers, which dire wolves do not manage. Its pelt, skull, and teeth would buy a season behind the walls, and the Council of Captains will pay either way to have the camps cutting again. Comfort and timber have sent both parties into the trees."
Brooding aftermath with the hunter still near.
Do not draw:
* Do not portray the boar: a barely seen shape only, no tusks in close-up.
* No carcass and no giant forest Avatar.
* Not the Bandit's Toll (this is a camp, not a trail barricade).
* No gore on the camp.
```

### 2. Scenario 2: The Whispering Grove

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-2-THE-WHISPERING-GROVE`

```text
SCENARIO 2: THE WHISPERING GROVE - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Whispering Grove, an ancient Greenmark clearing older than Greythorn, with humming standing stones where pale lights move on moonless nights and farmers leave bread as part of the tithe, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Night scene in a forest clearing seen from the tree edge; three tall standing stones dominate the middle, small pale lights drifting between them, a loaf of bread on the nearest stone.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Three mossy standing stones with spiral weathering, no letters
* Tiny pale lights as untouched circles in dense black hatching
* Loaf of bread and a cloth bundle at the foot of a stone
* Ring of oaks and undergrowth around the clearing
* Fine concentric ripples in the air around the stones to show the hum, drawn as thin rings
* Frost-free moss circle in the grass
* A lantern abandoned at the treeline
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* A single farmer in a hooded cloak walks away from the grove without looking back, small, at the far edge
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The grove is older than Greythorn and older than the western forest road that skirts it. The standing stones hum when no wind passes, and pale lights move between them on moonless nights. Greythorn's farmers leave bread there and do not look back. Scholars call it a site of power. The Council of Captains calls it part of the tithe, and pays it without complaint."
Hushed, reverent unease.
Do not draw:

* Not the Greenmark climate image (this is a clearing at night with stones).
* Not Maelthorn's offering clearing.
* No glow rays; lights are paper-white points.
```

### 3. Scenario 3: The Bandit's Toll

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-3-THE-BANDIT-S-TOLL`

```text
SCENARIO 3: THE BANDIT'S TOLL - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Bandit's Toll, a Greenmark scenario where a toll crew has claimed a bend of the western forest road, the artery carrying Deepwarden iron, Reedmarch salt and grain, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.

View along the forest trail toward a makeshift log barricade at a bend; waiting stolen wagons behind it and bandits lounging. The viewer stands on the road with a caravan queue behind, in the foreground, small.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Barricade of logs, upturned carts and spikes across the trail
* Prices nailed to the trees as crude boards smeared with dark marks, no letters
* Palisade of caravans waiting far behind with wagons circled
* Stolen wagons with broken barrels and iron ingots

HUMAN ACTIVITY

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The western forest road carries Deepwarden iron, Reedmarch salt, and grain between the Bastions, and it is the one artery Greythorn cannot do without. A toll crew has claimed a bend of the trail and nailed its prices to the trees in stolen blood. Caravans wait behind palisades while bread grows dear inside Greythorn's walls. The Council of Captains has stamped a bounty and sent it out with anyone hungry enough to answer."
Menace and sullen commerce.
Do not draw:
* Not the Spider Silk Route ravine or the Emissary ambush camp.

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-5-THE-AMBUSHED-EMISSARY`

```text
SCENARIO 5: THE AMBUSHED EMISSARY - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Ambushed Emissary, a Greenmark scenario where an emissary carrying documents sealed under two rival Bastion laws vanished on the western forest road and is now held in a makeshift bandit camp deep in the wood, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION

SUBJECT DETAILS
* Makeshift camp of hide lean-tos, a smoking fire pit and a rough palisade of stakes (cover only, not a fort)
* Emissary in fine but mud-stained robes, hands bound to a post, a satchel strap across their chest
* Forest-road reeve in half-armour with a longsword at the hip and a hand crossbow
* Clues in the foreground: a broken cart wheel, a snapped sealed document tube with no readable writing, torn cloth caught on a thorn, a cut rope hanging from a branch
* Tall oaks with small clearings and patches of undergrowth
* Dusk sky ruled lightly, crows returning to roost
* Boot tracks and wagon ruts leading off toward the road
Must be shown exactly:
* The emissary looks like a courier or diplomat
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Two bandits sit at the fire, one sharpening a blade
* The reeve stands over the emissary, arms folded
* Two tiny watchers crouch in the ferns at the bottom edge
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"An emissary vanished on the western forest road carrying documents sealed under two rival Bastion laws. One patron in Greythorn wants the courier found breathing. Another, further off, wants the satchel and does not ask after the courier. The deep wood has kept worse secrets, and the Hollow Quarter is not telling."
Tense, furtive espionage in the dark wood.
Do not draw:
* Not the Bandit's Toll barricade or the road ambush itself.
* No readable seals or documents.
* No violence against the emissary.
* Not an adventurer: the emissary is a courier or diplomat.
```

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The ruin does not keep time honestly. Glyphs flare and gutter. Soot drifts uphill in the corners. Stone that bore a traveller's weight at dawn may open beneath them by noon. Relics surface when the place remembers them and sink before anyone can make a clean claim, and the Sable Watch will register whatever is carried back to Sablekeep, whether the finder wishes it registered or not."
Disorienting, slow dread.
Do not draw:
* Not a desert; this is an Ashlands ruin, overcast and soot-dark.
* No clocks or hourglasses.
* Not the Fallen Observatory.
* No creature.
```

### 10. Scenario 4: The Sealed Archive

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-4-THE-SEALED-ARCHIVE`

```text
SCENARIO 4: THE SEALED ARCHIVE - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Sealed Archive, an Ashlands scenario under a fractured basilica containing tablets, scrolls, debt records and sermons the Sable Watch would rather burn, kept in order by something older, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.

SUBJECT DETAILS

* Shelves heaped with tablets, scrolls and ledgers, newer ones gnawed by scavengers
* Rats and scattered paper in the foreground
* Dust shown as dense stipple
* Lectern in the hall holding a closed codex, outlined with fine lines
* A suspiciously neat pile of stacked books beside the lectern
* Collapsed shelving and a sconce hanging crooked
Must be shown exactly:
* The book pile reads as books
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Two small intruders with a shuttered lantern edge along the corridor wall
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:

Scavengers have gnawed through the newer shelves. Something older keeps the inner stacks in order, and it has waited longer than Sablekeep has stood for someone to disturb them."
Choked silence and watchful order.
Do not draw:

* No creature shape in the book pile.
* No readable writing on tablets or scrolls.

### 12. Scenario 6: The Iron-Vein Vault

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-6-THE-IRON-VEIN-VAULT`

```text

Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of the Iron-Vein Vault, an Ashlands vault sealed for two generations with Karnessa Iron-Vein's blood on its iron door, now cracked by tremors as something below pushes, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:

Tremors along the imperial roads have cracked the outer binding, and something below is pushing. Sablekeep sends one party to reinforce the seal before whatever waits finishes its work; a rival patron sends another to open the vault and price what was sealed there. The vault does not care about either contract."
Held breath at the point of breaking.

There is no ocean for eighty miles."

## Stronghold rooms (11)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Interior or architectural view of the described stronghold room. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. 11.1 Campaign Overview

`ART-CAM-11-1-CAMPAIGN-OVERVIEW`

```text
11.1 CAMPAIGN OVERVIEW - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a hand-inked black-and-white illustration of a Stronghold, a claim driven into a dying world with coin, fear, salvage and stubborn labour, built of stones from ruined shrines, watch-keeps and broken bridges, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a skilled human illustrator inked the interior art of a classic 1970s-1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook: dip pen, brush and India ink on paper.
COMPOSITION
Three-quarter view of a stronghold under construction on a hill; workers haul shrine stones and broken bridge spans up a ramp, scaffolding around a half-built keep.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Half-built keep with scaffolding and ropes
* Reused masonry: a carved saint's head and a pier of a broken bridge span
* Ramp with a sled and rollers dragging a block
* Workers: debt-bound craftsmen, mules, a deserter soldier
* Timber crane or windlass
* Half-finished palisade and a ditch
* Stacked salvaged blocks and a mason's hut
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* A dozen workers haul stones up the ramp
* Others on the scaffolding set stones
* A guard stands on a wall section
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"A Stronghold is not a castle in the noble sense. No ancient bloodline sanctifies it. No bard remembers its founding. It is a claim driven into a dying world with coin, fear, salvage, and stubborn labour. Its stones come from ruined shrines, abandoned watch-keeps, broken bridges, and walls that failed other people first. Its gates are paid for with burial gold, bounty coin, chapel debt, and the last wages of the dead."
Stubborn labour at the edge of ruin.
Do not draw:
* Not a finished stronghold at dusk.
* Not a noble castle.
* No monsters.
* No readable plans or text.
```
